1991 CENSUS - Programme of Census Reports 
Preliminary 
1.1 This report was published in July 1991 and contained provisional figures of the population and private households for 
each of the 26 local government districts with a brief statistical commentary. 
Summary 
1.2 The Summary Report was published in October 1992 and contains information for Northern Ireland and for each of the 
26 local government districts. The subjects covered include age distribution, sex, marital status, birthplace, religion, households, 
tenure, amenities, occupation density, availability of cars, communal establishments, economically active population, 
employment status, long-term illness, term-time address of students and schoolchildren, and Irish language. 
Belfast Urban Area 
1.3 The Belfast Urban Area Report was published in December 1992 and contains information to ward level for the Belfast 
Urban Area as defined by the Department of the Environment for Northern Ireland Town and Country Planning Service. The 
subjects covered include age distribution, sex, marital status, birthplace, religion, households, tenure, amenities, occupation 
density, availability of cars, communal establishments, economically active population, employment status, long-term illness, 
term-time address of students and schoolchildren, and Irish language. 
Religion 
1.4 This report was published in February 1993 and contains information at Northern Ireland level and some to local 
government districts on the main religious denominations. The subjects covered include population by age, sex, marital status, 
occupation, industry, and other characteristics. 
Economic Activity 
1.5 This report was published in April 1993 and contains tables giving the figures for both the economically active and 
economically inactive population. The employed population is analysed by industry and occupation classifications and relates 
to age, marital status, employment status, socio-economic groupings, area of workplace, etc. Most tables contain information 
at Northern Ireland level but some give details for local government districts. 
Workplace and Transport to Work 
1.6 This report was published in June 1993 and contains details of the employed population in relation to area of 
workplace/residence. Workplace movement by socio-economic group, occupation, industry, and means of transport to work are 
detailed to local government district level. 
Housing and Household Composition 
1.7 All tables in this report contain information at Northern Ireland level but some give details for local government districts. 
Tables include information on households, tenure, occupation density, car availability, age, sex, marital status, and relate 
household composition to social and other economic characteristics. 
Migration 
1.8 Numbers and characteristics of the population who changed their usual address in the year before Census day will be 
contained in this report. Migrant households will be detailed by age, sex, marital status, etc. Some tables will refer to Northern 
Ireland and others will be detailed to local government districts. 

Education 
1.9 This report will contain information at Northern Ireland level by age, sex, occupation, and industry by highest level of 
academic qualification. Also included v^ill be socio-economic group by religion and economic activity by marital status related 
to highest level of academic qualification. ^ 
Irish Language 
1.10 This report will contain information on the population aged 3 years and over, recorded as having knowledge of Irish 
language. The tables covered will include knowledge by age, sex, religion, economic position, and highest level of academic 
qualification to Northern Ireland level. 
VI 

2 Explanatory Notes and Definitions of terms used in the report 
Introduction 
2.1 The tables in this report deal mainly with private households and the composition of private households. They contain 
details on social and economic composition and other characteristics of private households. Numbers of persons, age, sex, 
marital status, etc., are given with some tables referring to selected groups within the household such as families, married 
couples, chief economic supporters, heads of the household, earners, dependent children, etc. The households are related to size, 
tenure, density of occupation, dwelling type, amenities, car availability, etc. 
2.2 Details are also given for the population in non-private establishments such as hotels, hospitals, nursing homes etc., where 
persons are classified by sex, age and marital status. 
Administrative Divisions 
2.3 The local government districts for which statistics are presented in this report are those in existence at the date of the 
Census. 
Population 
2.4 Census results are presented as cross tabulations, each counting a particular population, for example, people, households, 
families, or people in communal establishments. It is important to know exactly what is being counted, i.e. the population base. 
Information on whereabouts and usual address given for each person listed on the Census form can be used to derive the 
population base. 
2.5 The population present on Census night (present population) in an area is the count of all people recorded as 
spending Census night in the area regardless of whether or not this was where they usually lived; that is, all persons recorded 
on the Census H Forms for whom the whereabouts on Census night was given as the relevant household, plus all persons 
recorded as being in communal establishments. 
2.6 The usually resident population in an area is the count of those people recorded on the forms for the area as usually 
living *at this address' whether or not they are present on Census night. 
2.7 Shipping: Apart from houseboats (enumerated as private households), vessels were treated in a similar way to communal 
establishments with a listing form and individual return form for each person on the ship. Persons on naval vessels in Northern 
Ireland waters or ports on Census night were included as present in the home base port of the ship. Generally persons on other 
vessels were counted as present in the area in which the form was collected. 
2.8 For the 1991 Census there were some minor changes made to the enumeration of shipping. Vessels on coastal trips, 
fishing voyages and voyages between Great Britain and Northern Ireland were not enumerated. However, foreign registered 
ships in Northern Ireland ports, excluded in 1981, were enumerated. 
Campers, persons sleeping rough, etc. 
2.9 The present population also includes persons not enumerated in private households or communal establishments, for 
example, persons sleeping rough and persons enumerated in caravans or other non-permanent structures which were not the 
usual residence. 
Population covered in this Report 
2.10 Figures contained in this report refer to the usually resident population with the exception of Table 16. The figures in 
this table only include persons who were present in non-private establishments on Census night. 
vn 

Households 
2.11 A household is either one person living alone or a group of people (who may or may not be related) living at the same 
address with common housekeeping. Persons staying temporarily with the household are included. A group of people is treated 
as a household if there is any regular arrangement to share at least one meal a day. Two people who share one room, although 
each may have separate catering arrangements, are treated as one household. A household must have exclusive use of at least 
one room. 
2.12 Absent households are those households where all residents of the household were recorded as absent from their usual 
address on Census night and at which no other person was present. 
Dwellings 
2.13 Dwellings are defined as 'structurally separate accommodation' and are classified into permanent and non-permanent 
accommodation. 
2.14 Permanent acconmiodation includes: 
- a whole house or bungalow, whether detached, semi-detached or terraced; 
- the whole of a purpose built flat or maisonette including any situated in a commercial building (for example in an 
office building or hotel or over a shop) or in a block of flats; 
- part of a concerted or shared house, bungalow or flat with a separate or shared entrance into the building. This 
includes a one roomed flatlet, one room or a bedsit not self-contained, a self-contained flat or accommodation with 
two or more rooms, or two or more rooms not self-contained. 
2.15 Figures on self-contained accommodation in this report reflect the responses ticked at Panel A boxes 7 or 8 and Panel 
HI boxes 1 or 3 of the Census form. This category of accommodation is defined as: 
- a one ropmed flatlet with a private bath or shower, WC and kitchen facilities i.e. Census form Panel A boxes 7 or 
8 and Panel HI box 1; 
- a flat or accommodation with 2 or more rooms having bath or shower, WC and kitchen facilities all behind its own 
private door i.e. Census form Panel A boxes 7 or 8 and Panel HI box 3. 
2.16 Figures on not self-contained accommodation reflect the responses ticked at Panel A boxes 7 or 8 and Panel HI 
boxes 2 or 4 of the Census form. This category of accommodation is defined as: 
- one roomi or bedsit with access to bathroom, WC or kitchen facility through shared hall, landing or stairway open 
to other households i.e. Census form Panel A boxes 7 or 8 and Panel HI box 2; 
- 2 or more rooms with access to these rooms, bathroom, WC or kitchen facilities through shared hall, landing or 
stairway open to other households i.e. Census form Panel A boxes 7 or 8 and Panel HI box 4. 
2.17 Non-permanent accommodation includes: 
- caravans or mobile structures and such non-permanent structures as huts, shacks, tents, etc., are treated as dwellings 
only if they were some person's usual residence on Census night. If such structures were vacant on Census night 
they were excluded. 
2.18 A vacant dwelling is one which was fit or ready for habitation but was awaiting occupancy - this type of dwelling 
would invariably be unfurnished. Derelict or uninhabitable dwellings are excluded. 
Shared dwellings 
2.19 Shared dwellings include both those dwellings with a separate entrance into a building, and those sharing an entrance. 
Shared dwelling (separate entrance) 
This is a dwelling with a separate entrance into a building comprising one room or bedsit not self-contained, or two 
or more rooms that are not self-contained i.e. Census form Panel A box 7 and Panel HI 2 or 4. 
viii 

Shared dwelling (shared entrance) 
This is a dwelling sharing an entrance into a building comprising one room or bedsit not self-contained, or two or 
more rooms that are not self-contained i.e. Census form Panel A box 8 and Panel HI 2 or 4. 
Unshared dwellings 
2.20 Unshared dwellings include: 
- a whole house or bungalow that is detached, semi-detached or terraced (including end of terrace) i.e. Census form 
Panel A boxes 2, 3 or 4; 
- the whole of a purpose built flat or maisonette in a commercial building (for example in an office building or hotel 
or over a shop) or in a block of flats i.e. Census form Panel A boxes 5 or 6; 
- part of a converted or shared house, bungalow or flat with a separate entrance into the building comprising a one 
roomed flatlet self-contained, or a self-contained flat or accommodation with two or more rooms i.e. Census form 
Panel A box 7 and Panel HI boxes 1 or 3. 
Unshared dwellings (shared entrance) 
2.21 Unshared dwellings (shared entrance) include: 
- part of a converted or shared house, bungalow or fiat sharing an entrance into a building comprising a one roomed 
flatlet self-contained i.e. Census form Panel A box 8 and Panel HI box 1; 
- part of a converted or shared house, bungalow or flat sharing an entrance into a building comprising a self-contained 
flat or accommodation with two or more rooms i.e. Census form Panel A box 8 and Panel HI box 3. 
Communal Establishments 
2.22 People were enumerated in a variety of communal establishments (non-private establishments) such as hotels, boarding 
schools, hospitals, prisons, religious communities, etc. The population enumerated in communal establishments plus persons 
sleeping rough and campers is referred to as the papulation in non-private households. It mcludes all those present in the 
establishment on Census night and all who arrived on Monday 22 April 1991 and who had not been included on a Census form 
as present elsewhere. Non-resident staff/personnel on duty in the premises on Census night are not included. 
2.23 Separate buildings in the grounds of an establishment for the accommodation of private households, such as a doctor's 
house or caretaker's cottage in the grounds of a hospital, are classed as private residences and not as part of the communal 
establishment. People occupying flats or suites of rooms within the main building of an establishment are treated as part of the 
main establishment and included on the L form. Service families or civilians living in married quarters of the Armed Services 
are treated as private households whether the quarters were located within or outside the boundaries of the service 
establishment. 
2.24 Annexes to communal establishments are treated as part of the main establishment if located in the same enumeration 
district or if meals were taken at the main establishment even though the annexe was in a different enumeration district. 
Establishment classification 
2.25 The categories are as follows: 
1. Hotels, etc. 
2. NHS hospitals and statutory homes (psychiatric). 
3. NHS hospitals and statutory homes (others). 
4. Non-NHS hospitals and statutory homes (psychiatric). 
5. Non-NHS hospitals and statutory homes (others). 
6. Homes for the old and disabled. 
7. Children's homes. 
8. Educational establishments. 
9. Places of detention. 
10. Defence establishments including naval vessels and service hospitals. 
11. Civilian ships, boats and barges. 
12. Hostels and lodging houses. 
13. Other miscellaneous establishments. 
14. Campers and persons sleeping rough. 
ix 

Residence classification 
2.26 Persons enumerated in groups 1 to 9 and 12 to 14 above are given one of the 3 following residence classification codes: 
(i) Managers and staff, 
(ii) Boarders, patients and inmates, 
(iii) Others. 
2.27 All persons present in groups 10 and 11 are classed under category (ii). Boarders include hotel guests, persons in 
lodgings and military personnel in communal establishments including naval vessels. 
Age 
2.28 Age is the completed years of age at Census date, as derived from the date of birth question. 
Pensionable age 
2.29 Pensionable age is the minimum age at which a person may receive a National Insurance Retirement Pension - 60 for 
women and 65 for men. 
Usual Address 
2.30 In most cases the toswer to the usual address question is straightforward. However, students and schoolchildren away 
from home during term-time were to regard their home address as their usual address. Boarders were to decide whether they 
considered as their usual address, the address of the relevant household. Household heads, living away from home for part of 
the week, were to regard their home address as their usual address. 
2.31 Persons who failed to answer the relevant questions appear as part of the usually resident population in the area in which 
they were enumerated and are included as part of a usually resident private household. 
Visitors to Northern Ireland 
2.32 Visitors to Northern Ireland are those present at the relevant household address on Census night whose usual address 
is outside Northern Ireland. 
Economic Activity 
2.33 The economic activity tables describe some of the economic characteristics of the population. The characteristics include 
both those obtained directly from census questions, for example, economic position, and occupation, and others derived from 
combining answers to census questions on occupation and industry, for example, employment status. Persons on a Government 
employment or training scheme are regarded as economically active in employment. However, while information about the 
particular scheme was not collected in the Census, persons on these schemes are included as a separate group in tables which 
give details on economic position and occupation. 
2.34 Only specified groups were required to answer some of the economic activity questions and this is reflected in the 
population to which tables refer. Thus: 
(a) Tables relating to economic position include only persons aged 16 years and over. 
(b) Tables relating to occupation cover the economically active. 
(c) Tables relating to employment status cover persons in employment. 

Economic Position 
2.35 The classification by economic position is as follows: 
Economically active 
In employment means having a job during the week ended 20 April 1991. Temporary, part-time or casual 
employment is included. Persons absent from their employment due to holiday, strikes, lock-outs, short-time working 
or temporary stoppages are regarded as in employment. Persons off work sick are regarded as in employment if their 
jobs are waiting for them on return. 
Out of employment means seeking work or prevented by temporary sickness from seeking work or waiting to take 
up a job already accepted. 
Economically inactive 
Looking after the home means having full-time responsibility for looking after the home. 
Long-term sick/disabled means being unable to work because of long-term sickness or disability. 
Retired means wholly retired from employment and no longer seeking paid work. 
Students are persons in full-time attendance at an educational establishment irrespective of whether or not they may 
have part-time or seasonal employment. 
Others are persons economically inactive and include persons in unpaid work in a family business including a shop 
or farm, or looking after the house or family, or in voluntary work, or never in employment and not seeking work, 
and those of independent means. 
Socio-economic Groups 
2.36 Classification by socio-economic groups was.introduced in 1951 and extensively amended in 1961. The classification 
aims to bring together people with jobs of similar social and economic status. The allocation of occupied persons to socio­
economic groups is determined by considering their employment status and occupation. 
The socio-economic groups with brief definitions are: 
(1) Employers and managers in central and local government, industry, commerce, etc. - large establishments 
1.1 Employers in industry, commerce, etc. 
Persons who employ others in non-agricultural enterprises employing 25 or more persons. 
1.2 Managers in central and local government, industry, commerce, etc. 
Persons who generally plan and supervise in non-agricultural enterprises employing 25 or more persons. 
(2) Employers and managers in industry, commerce, etc. - small establishments 
2.1 Employers in industry, commerce, etc. - small establishments. As in 1.1 but in establishments employing fewer 
than 25 persons. 
2.2 Managers in industry, commerce, etc. - small establishments. As in 1.2 but in establishments employing fewer 
than 25 persons. 
(3) Professional workers - self-employed 
Self-employed persons engaged in work normally requiring qualifications of university degree standard. 
(4) Professional workers - employees 
Employees engaged in work normally requiring qualifications of university degree standard. 
XI 

(5) Intermediate non-manual workers 
5.1 Ancillary workers and artists. 
Employees engaged in non-manual occupations ancillary to the professions, not normally requiring 
qualifications of university degree standard; persons engaged in artistic work and not employing others therein. 
Self-employed nurses, medical auxiliaries, teachers, work study engineers and technicians are included. 
5.2 Foremen and supervisors non-manual. 
Employees (other than managers) engaged in occupations included in group 6, who formally and immediately 
supervise others engaged in such occupations. 
(6) Junior non-manual workers 
Employees, not exercising general planning or supervisory powers, engaged in clerical, sales and non-manual 
communications occupations, excluding those who have additional and formal supervisory functions (these are 
included in group 5.2). 
(7) Personal service workers 
Employees engaged in service occupations caring for food, drink, clothing and other personal needs. 
(8) Foremen and supervisors - manual 
Employees (other than managers) who formally and immediately supervise others engaged in manual occupations, 
whether or not themselves engaged in such occupations. 
(9) Skilled manual workers 
Employees engaged in manual occupations which require considerable and specific skills. 
(10) Semi-skilled manual workers ^ 
Employees engaged in manual occupations which require slight but specific skills. 
(11) UnskUled manual workers 
Other employees engaged in manual occupations. 
(12) Own account workers (other than professional) 
i 
Self-employed persons engaged in any trade, personal service or manual occupation not normally requiring training 
of university degree standard and having no employees other than family workers. 
(13) Farmers - employers and managers 
Persons who own, rent or manage farms, market gardens or forests, employing people other than family workers in 
the work of the enterprise. 
(14) Farmers - own account 
Persons who own or rent farms, market gardens or forests and having no employees other than family workers. 
(15) Agricultural \yorkers 
Persons engaged in tending crops, animals, game or forests, or operating agricultural or forestry machinery. 
(16) Members of armed forces 
(17) Inadequately described and not stated occupations 
xu 

Part-time Workers 
2.37 Part-time workers were persons in a job in the week preceding Census day, in which the hours worked, excluding any 
overtime, were usually 30 hours or less per week. 
Employment status 
2.38 The classification of employment status is as follows: 
Self-employed 
1. With employees, 
2. Without employees. 
Employees 
1. Managers. 
(a) extra large establishments. 
(b) large establishments. 
(c) small establishments. 
2. Foremen and supervisors. 
(a) manual. 
(b) non-manual. 
3. Professional employees. 
4. Other employees. 
2.39 Information on occupation is used to distinguish the various types of employees and information on name and business 
of employer to distinguish the size of the establishment Extra large establishments are those classified as having 500 or more 
employees. Large establishments are those having between 25 and 499 employees. Small establishments are those with fewer 
than 25 employees. 
Head of Household 
2.40 The head of the household was the person entered in the first column of the Census form provided that person was aged 
16 years or over and usually resident at 'this address'. 
2.41 If one of these conditions was not met, the first person aged 16 years or over to be entered on the form and recorded 
as usually resident at 'this address' was classified as the head. In the last resort the oldest resident under 16 years of age was 
taken as the head. No head was identified in households consisting entirely of visitors. 
Chief Economic Supporter 
2.42 In a number of tables households are classified by the characteristics of the Chief Economic Supporter (CES) and not 
by those of the head of household, although in the majority of cases the stated head of the household was also the CES. 
2.43 In determining the CES only persons stated to be usually resident at 'this address' were considered. The first person 
entered on the form as economically active was regarded as the CES. If there was no person entered as economically active 
then the oldest retired person was treated as the CES. If there were no retired persons then the oldest person was selected as 
the CES. 
Family 
2.44 A family is (a) a married couple with or without never-married child(ren), (b) a father or mother with his or her never-
married child(ren) or (c) grandparent(s) with grandchild(ren) if there are no apparent parents of the grandchild(ren) usually 
resident in the household. 
xm 

2.45 In this definition there is no age hmit to the child. Persons not in a family are those persons in the household who could 
not be allocated to a family on the above definitions. 
Dependent Child 
2.46 A dependent child is a person aged 0-15 in a household; or a person aged 16-19 never-married, in full-time education 
and economically inactive. The additional qualification 'and economically inactive' has been added to the 1981 Census 
definition (which also included persons aged 19-25 who were never-married and classified as a student) from the question on 
economic activity. 
Non-dependent Child 
2.47 A non-dependent child is a child in a family who is never-married, aged 16 years or over, not in full-time education 
and economically active., 
Husband and Wife 
2.48 A husband is the male of a married couple and the wife is the female of a married couple. 
Earners 
2.49 The number of tamers in a household or family is the number of persons in the household or family classified as 
economically active. 
Family Household 
2.50 The classification of family household relates only to- usually resident persons in the household and is as follows:-
Non-family Household 
1. One person. 
2. Two or more persons. 
One-family Household 
Married couple, no children, no others. 
Married couple, no children, with others. 
Married couple, with children, no others. 
Married couple, with children, with others. 
Lone parent, with children, no others. 
Lone parent, with children, with others. 
Lone grandparent, with grandchildren, no others. 
Lone grandparent, with grandchildren, with others. 
Two-family Household 
1. Related, no others. 
2. Related, with others. 
3. Not related, no others. 
4. Not related, with others. 
Three or more family household. 
XIV 

Rooms 
2.51 Private households were asked to count the number of rooms in their accommodation, but not to count the following: 
Kitchens less than 2 metres (6 feet 6 inches) wide, bathrooms or toilets. 
2.52 In unshared dwellings the head of the household provided information. In shared dwellings the enumerator was 
instructed to enquire of each household in the dwelling if any room was shared by another household. If any room was shared 
by 2 or more households the enumerator was further instructed to identify separate households where appropriate. 
2.53 In the tables classifying households by rooms occupied, any room shared by 2 or more households is allocated to one 
household to avoid the use of fractions and to ensure that the total number of rooms in the dwelling is not altered. 
Tenure 
2.54 Heads of households were required to indicate under the following headings how they occupied their accommodation. 
As an owner occupier: 
1. Through mortgage or loan. 
2. Owning the property outright. 
By renting, rent free or by lease: 
3. From a public authority (e.g. NI Housing Executive). 
4. From a housing association or charitable trust. 
5. Unfurnished from a private landlord, company or other organisation. 
6. Furnished from a private landlord, company or other organisation. 
In some other way 
If the Census form indicated more than one form of tenure but gave no further information the tenure was assigned 
to this category. Where no tenure was stated on the Census form, the accommodation was similarly assigned. 
2.55 A shared dwelling was classified according to the tenure stated by the main household. 
Density of Occupation 
2.56 The density of occupation of a private household is the ratio of the number of persons in a private household with usual 
residents to the number of rooms occupied by the private household. The following ratios of density are used: 
Over 1.5 persons per room. 
Over one and up to 1.5 persons per room. 
Over 0.75 and up to one person per room. 
0.5 and over and up to 0.75 persons per room. 
Less than 0.5 persons per room. 
Household Amenities 
2.57 The amenities on which information was collected in respect of each private household are listed below. In the case 
of fixed bath or shower and flush toilets the householder was required to indicate whether individual amenities were used only 
by the household, shared with another household or were not available. 
(a) A fixed bath or shower permanently connected to a water supply and waste pipe. 
(b) A flush toilet (WC) with entrance inside the building. 
2.58 Additional information on amenities was collected in respect of each private household. Householders were required 
to indicate whether the household had use of the following amenities: 
XV 

Central heating 
Central heating includes night storage heaters, warm air or under-floor heating, whether actually used or not. It is 
categorised as follows: 
L All living rooms and bedrooms centrally heated. 
2. Some (not all) living rooms and bedrooms centrally heated. 
3. No living rooms or bedrooms centrally heated. 
Water supply 
1. Public supply piped into house, 
2. Public supply at a standpipe. 
3. Other. 
Domestic sewage disposal 
1. Public sewer. 
2. Septic tank/cesspit for use only by the household. 
3. Septic tank/cesspit for use also by another household. 
4. Dry closet/chemical toilet. 
Cars and Vans 
2.59 The number of Cars and vans normally available for use by the household include any car or van provided by employers 
but exclude vans used solely for the carriage of goods. 
Presentation Conventions 
2.60 A dash (-) indicates that the entry is zero. A blank indicates that a particular combination of line and column is 
impossible. In the presentation of rates, a rate shown as 0 indicates that the actual rate is less than one half of a unit but is not 
zero. 
H Form 
2.61 For reference purposes the H form used for private households in the 1991 Census is produced at the end of this report.

H Form for Private Households 
To the Head or Joint Heacts or members of the Household aged 
16 or over " •-. >•• . '-^ - • • 
Please complete thjs form for all members of the houselKild, Inchidlng 
chiMren, and have it ready for collection on Monday 22nd April. Your Census 
Enumerator will call to collect it then or soon afterwards and will help you with the 
form if you have any difficulties. The enclosed leaflet explains why the Census is 
necessary and how the information is used. 
Completion of the form is compulsory under the Census Act (Northern Ireland)1969. If 
you refuse to complete it, or give false information, you may have to pay a fine of up to 
^00. There is no penalty for refusing to state religious denomination as this is a 
voluntary question. 
Your ai^iwers wUl be treated in strict con^dence and used only to pitxhice 
statistics. No names and addresses will be put into the computer; only the postcode 
will be entered. The forms will be kept securely and treated as confidential. No 
information about sramed indivldaals will be jessed by the Onsus OfBce to any 
other Government Department or to any other authority or person. 
Anyone using or disclosing census information improperly will be liable to 
prosecution. For example, it would be improper for you to pass on to someone else 
information which you have been given in confidence by a visitor to enable you to 
complete the Census Form. 
If any member of the household aged 16 or over does not wish you, or another 
member of the household, to see their information, please ask the Enumerator for an 
Individual Form with an envelope. 
After completing the form, please sign the declaration on the last page. 
Thank you for your co-operation. 
Please read these instructions before filling in this form 
A Household: 
A botisebold comprises either one person Jiving alone or a group of people (not 
necessarily related) living at the same address unth common housekeeping -
that is, sharing at least one meal a day or sharing a living room or sitting 
room. 
People staying temporarily uHth the household are included. 
W" If there is more than one household in this building, answer for your 
household only.

In answering the rest of the questions please include: 
^ every person who spends census night (21-22 April) in this household, 
Indoding anyone staying temporarily. 
^ any other people who are usually members of the household but on census night are 
absent on holiday, at school or college, or for any other reason, even if you know they 
are being included on another census form elsewhere. 
^ anyone who anives here on Monday 22nd ApriJ who was in Noiihem Ireland on 
Sunday 21st April and who has not been included as present on another census form. 
^ any baby bom before 22 April, even if still in hospital. If not yet given a nsune write 
BABY and the surname. 
^ Write the names in BLOCK CAFTTALS starting -with the head or a joint head 
of household.

nil Please read A below, tick the box that applies and follow the instruction by the box ticked. 
This will teU you which questions to answer for each person. 
Did the person have a paid job fest week or do unpaid work in a 
family business, including a shop or farm (any of the boxes 1, 2, 3,4 or 5 ticked a 
question 15)? 
Has the person had a paid job within the last 10 years? 
How many hours a week does or did the pereon usually work in his or her main job? 
Bo not count overttme or meal breaks. 
17 Occupation, 
Please give the full title of the person's present or last job and describe the main things 
he /she does or did in the job. 
At a, give the fijll title by which the job is known, for esample: 'packing machinist*; 
'poultry processor*; 'jig andjlool fitter"; 'supervisor of typists'; 'accounts clerk'; rather 
than general titles like 'machinist'; 'proce^ worker"; 'supervisor' or 'clerk'. Give rank 
or grade if the peraon has one. 
At b, write down the main things the person actually does or did in the job. If possible 
ask him/her to say what these things are and write them down. 
Armed Forces - enter 'commissioned officer' or 'other rank' as appropriate at a, and 
leave b blank. 
Civil Servante - give grade at a, and at b state discipline or specialism, for example: 
'electrical engineef; 'accountant'; 'chemist'; 'administrator'. 
Other Public OfiQdals - give rank or grade at a and description of duties at b. 
18 ^rnploytr (\i self employed j»ive 
At 3, please give the name of the employer. Give the tracing name if one is used. Do 
not use abbreviations. 
Atb, describe clearly what the employer (or the person if self-employed) makes or 
does (or did). 
Armed Forces - write 'Armed Forces' at a and leave b blank. For a member of the 
Armed Forces of a counliy other than the UK - add the name of the country. 
CivU Servants - give name of Department at a and write 'Government Department' at 
b 
Other Publk: Offlcbds - give name of employing authority at a and department in 
which employed at b.

^ every person who spends census night (21-22 April) in this household,

1^ any other people who are usually members of the household but on census night are

^ anyone who arrives here on Monday 22nd April who was in Northern Ireland on

Please give the ftill address of the person's place of work.

l' 
For a person not reporting daily to a fixed address, tick box 1.

peison's daily journey to work is normally made. 
For a person using d^erent m&ms of transport on different days, show the means 
most often used. 
Car or van includes three-wheeled cars and motor caravans.
